The deferring arm's wording stopped presupposing the child would become active | Lead's own mutation: making `pairingRedeemable` ignore the status and the deletion flag reds **19 web AND 9 mobile**. 24 new cases in `apps/mobile/src/family/pair-device.test.ts`. The agent ran its `heldPairingCode` mutation twice, before and after its own change, to show the original defect restored | none |
| BUG-425 | Low / spec P4 — a shared sentence contradicting one of the two controls it serves | closed | the lead, by RENDERING the screen in a real browser and looking at it | `IDENTITY_REQUIREMENT_COPY.dateOfBirth` read "Enter your date of birth as year-month-day, from 1900 onwards — for example 1990-04-12." That is true of the PHONE, whose control is a free-text field with a `YYYY-MM-DD` placeholder, and FALSE of the PORTAL, whose control is `<input type="date">` — a native picker rendering `mm/dd/yyyy` in a US locale that never takes that string as typed text. So a parent who submitted with the date empty read an instruction, in red, describing a format the control beside it does not use | — | The sentence now names the VALUE — "Add your own date of birth, from 1900 onwards — the one printed on the ID." — and each control keeps teaching its own format, which both already did (the portal's picker by being one, the phone's field by its placeholder). NOT split into two sentences: that is the drift these constants exist to prevent, and this is the MIRROR of BUG-421..424 rather than another instance — not two copies of one sentence, but one sentence covering two things that genuinely differ | `identity.test.ts` `[BUG-425]`: a property that NO requirement sentence teaches an input format, plus that the date one still carries what is genuinely shared (whose date, the floor, and that it is the one printed on the ID). Mutation: restoring the format sentence reds 2; moving `BIRTH_YEAR_MIN` off 1900 reds 1 | Found only by looking. No test could have seen it: every test asserts this constant against itself, so the sentence and the expectation moved together (L-067). The four screenshot states are in the session scratchpad, not committed |
| BUG-426 | Medium / record accuracy — a wrong gate in the record the owner plans from | closed | the lead, answering "what is left to get to beta" by reading the records rather than from memory | `docs/Beta_Readiness.md`'s three-gates table said "the web build fails without `VITE_LEGAL_REVIEWED=true`, an effective date and a support mailbox". The opposite is true: a build with the flag UNSET succeeds and ships a draft banner, and what throws is CLAIMING review without the evidence. The record therefore put counsel's sign-off on the critical path of the parent-only walkthrough, which it does not gate — and that walkthrough is the cheapest real information available about this product. A wrong gate in a readiness record is worse than a wrong comment in code: the owner plans from it, and this one would have delayed the first useful beta behind a lawyer for no reason | — | Both the gate row and the walkthrough section corrected, each naming the function that decides (`isLegalReviewed`, `DraftBanner`) so a reader verifies in one grep rather than trusting the sentence (the vagueness is what let the round-7 false sharing claims stand for a round) | Proved rather than read: built the portal with none of the three legal variables set — exit 0, draft banner in the bundle. The inverse case (the flag true with no effective date) is already covered by `apps/web/src/lib/config.test.ts` | Suggests the other gate rows deserve the same treatment: each says what enforces it, and only this one was checked by running it |
| BUG-427 | High / AC_PRIVACY — a child identifier on the wire to a third-party provider | closed | the lead, closing `ResponsesRequest.metadata` from `Record<string, string>` to the `ZdrSafeMetadata` allow-list | `packages/ai/src/ai.test.ts` built its request with `metadata: { child: 'pseudonymous-id' }`, and the type permitted it: `Record<string, string>`. `metadata` is sent in the request body and is the one field OpenAI documents as stored BESIDE the request, so it survives in places the prompt does not. Pseudonymous is not anonymous — a provider-side identifier that is stable per child is exactly what links one request to the next. The fixture is also the shape a developer copies, so the defect's real cost was the next call site, not this one. Nothing failed: the field's docblock said "pseudonymous ids only" and no test read it | "The docblock is the control" — rejected: a comment is not a compiler, and the one fixture in the repository already violated it | `metadata` is now `ZdrSafeMetadata` (`stage`, optional `prompt_version`), enforced three ways: by the type, by `assertZdrSafeMetadata` at the transport before any fetch, and by a test that reads this package's own fixtures for identifier-shaped keys. The fixture became `{ stage: 'coaching' }` | `zdr.test.ts` "no fixture in this package carries an identifier-shaped metadata key" (scans the package's sources) and "the allow-list refuses an identifier-shaped key"; mutation Z6 (opening the allow-list) reds 1 | The scanner reads `packages/ai` only; the same check over `apps/api` fixtures is worth adding when a route builds metadata itself |
| BUG-428 | High / AC_PRIVACY + AC_RELIABILITY — a refusal reclassified as a transport failure, so a forbidden request retried forever | closed | the lead, by writing the test that DRIVES the transport rather than reading the guard | The three ZDR guards (`assertApprovedProviderHost`, `assertZdrEligibleEndpoint`, `assertZdrSafeMetadata`) were placed as the first statements INSIDE `create`'s `try`. That `try`'s `catch` exists for fetch failures and returns `{ kind: 'error', status: null, retryable: true }` for anything thrown. So a request carrying a child's identifier did not stop: it was reported as a retryable network error, and the job runner — correctly, given that answer — retried it, forever, on work that could never succeed. The privacy control and the reliability control destroyed each other, and both looked right in isolation | "A guard that throws is self-evidently fail-closed" — rejected: fail-closed depends on who catches it. The throw was intact; the classification above it turned a permanent refusal into a transient one | The three assertions moved ABOVE the `try`, with a comment stating why they sit outside it. They are programming errors, not transport failures, and a programming error must reach the caller as a throw | `zdr.test.ts` "a forbidden endpoint is refused before any fetch is attempted" and "a metadata violation throws rather than returning a retryable error" (asserts `rejects`, and that `fetchImpl` was never called); mutation Z5b (removing the runtime metadata guard) reds 1 | The same shape exists wherever a guard was added inside an existing `try`; `moderation.ts`'s call site was checked and is outside its own (L-079) |
| BUG-429 | Medium / AC_LEARNING_03/04 — a child got less practice on their weakest skill because of their own id | closed | the lead's whole-repo gate, which reddened ONE case in 1203 under full-repo contention and passed on every isolated re-run | `apps/api/tests/learning-jobs.test.ts` "weights weak skills from homework evidence" expected at least 2 weak questions on `math.fractions_add_like` after three incorrect homework attempts, and got 1. Root cause is not the test: `generateCandidates` seeds itself with the practice set key, which contains the child's random uuid, and asked each skill for items THREE times (1 diagnostic, 2 standard, 1 accessible) with a `seen` set local to each call. A cross-call duplicate was then dropped by `uniqueByInstance` with nothing drawing again, so for about 13% of children a skill's pool held 3 candidates instead of 4 — and the weak part of their daily set could place only one question on the skill they were actually failing. Measured over 400 random child ids per grade: 4 candidates 86.75%, 3 candidates 13.25%, 2 candidates 0.25% (grade 4). "Deterministic for a given seed" was true and said nothing about this, because the seed is per-child (L-081) | "A flake under CPU contention" — rejected by measuring: the failure is seed-dependent, not time-dependent, and reproduces deterministically at `daily:child-10:2026-09-24` with no database and no load | `generateSkillItems` takes an `avoid` set that pre-seeds its `seen`, and `generateCandidates` keeps one set per SKILL across its three category draws. A collision now redraws inside the existing draw budget, where there is still something to draw, instead of being discarded after generation. `uniqueByInstance` stays as the backstop | `packages/domain/src/bank/bank.test.ts` "a child's id must not decide how many questions their weakest skill gets" — 40 fixed seeds, each asserted to yield the same per-skill count map and all four distinct candidates for a generated math skill. Both cases RED before the fix (`expected 3 to be 4`), green after; the probe over 1200 seeds went from 3 distinct pool sizes to one | The property test covers all six subjects at grades 1, 3, 5 and 8, which found the second half: `grammar.plural_nouns` has exactly FOUR words at grade 1, so the final `avoid`-constrained draw faced a 1-in-4 pool on a 12-draw budget and came up short for one child in fifty. The budget now covers the items already taken, and no skill varied across 3600 probed seeds at grades 0–8. RESIDUAL: that is about (3/4)^48 per constrained draw, a probability and not a proof — a word pool the size of the request is the sharp edge, and growing it is content work. Also worth asking which OTHER pinned tests are seed-dependent in the same way |
